# Changelog

Every release of `papers-experiments-core`. Versions follow semantic versioning with tags `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`; while the version is 0.x, a minor release may break interfaces, and its entry says which.

## 0.1.0 (unreleased)

The first release: what a study needs to run end to end on the core (agent loop, model backends, tools, prompts, records and replay, the campaign kernel, the statistics kernel, provenance and replication packages), with the Onedata platform layer: configuration of one space or several, token planes, tool-server launch, health, the sandbox and its data paths, the data gate, provisioning and placement, a complete reset between runs, platform probes, leased platform users, fault injection with target-matched treatment and day-0 checks. A study depends on it by tag and copies nothing; [`docs/study-guide.md`](docs/study-guide.md) shows how. What the release guarantees is the list of regressions at the end of this entry: each has a test on this tree, named beside it, whose docstring starts with the ID. An ID of [`docs/regressions.md`](docs/regressions.md) that is not in the list is not guaranteed yet.

**The live contract tests have run against the Compose federation of `deploy/compose-federation`, and what each run established is in the dated day-0 reports and facts files beside it, not in this entry.** The platform layer is tested against fakes of the Onedata API, of the MCP server and of the Compose services the fault injector stops and starts, and the contract tests in `tests/live/` skip unless `EXPCORE_LIVE_PLATFORM` names a deployment, the one that takes a provider down also unless `EXPCORE_LIVE_FAULTS=1`. That federation runs a 25.0 development build, not the released tag (the entry on its build, under Added). The day-0 checks that change state, `read_only` and `round_trip`, passed on it in the day-0 reports, and the provider-outage check `provider_outage`, with the fault injector, a real sandbox in mount mode and the real tool server, passed on it on 12 Nov (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-12-federation-facts-provider-outage-rerun.md`). A feature that has no live run yet says so under Known limits.

### Added

- An analysis may build its plugin without the study's platform (R17; `build_analysis(context) -> StudyPlugin`, `expcore.study.analysis_plugin`). A plugin module may define it beside `build`: `analyze`, `report`, `regenerate` and a package's check build with it when it is there and with `build` when it is not, so a batch run on an HPC allocation can be analysed after copy-back, where the rendered federation `build` needs is gone and only the scorers matter. A run never calls it (`load_plugin` does not look for it, and a test fails on any other reference to the analysis loader). It is checked for its scorers alone. The campaign fingerprint already records the run plugin's `scorer_digest`; an analysis built by `build_analysis` is refused, before any record is scored, unless its scorers have that digest, and the refusal names the scorers, never a digest or a version. A snapshot that records no valid digest is refused too (every fingerprint the core writes holds one, so such a store was changed or is damaged); the outputs are byte for byte the same whichever builder served the analysis, and a module without `build_analysis` is loaded exactly as before. With it defined, a scorer changed since the run is refused, unless the analysis is asked to re-score on purpose with `analyze --rescore` and `report --rescore` (`allow_rescore=True` of `analyse_study` and `regenerate`), for a scorer corrected after an HPC batch, where `build` cannot run: the flag relaxes only a digest that differs, never a store that records none, and the re-score is recorded as R13 records one (the corrected scorers' versions in `scores.json`, the `scorers` of `analysis.json` and the report's `Scorers` line) with the digest the campaign recorded and the digest of the scorers used, in `analysis.json` (`rescore`) and one line of the report's provenance. With the campaign's own scorers, or without `build_analysis`, the flag changes no output. Which scorer changed is not named: the store holds one digest of them all. A replication package of a re-scored batch is refused, as its build and check pass no flag. See [`docs/interfaces.md`](docs/interfaces.md) and the study guide, section 5.
- The federation's own services, observed over a window, the same on Compose and on HPC (O86; `expcore_onedata.fedwatch.FederationWatch`, `watch_for`). `read()` at a window's start and `window(start)` at its end give, for the zone and each provider, the service's state as observed (Compose: the container's id, start, `RestartCount`, health and exit; HPC: its Slurm step by its exact id through `squeue -s` and `sacct`, `deploy/hpc-federation/hpcobserve.py`, `hpcslurm.Slurm.step_observed`) and its own `GET /configuration` answer at both ends. A restart inside the window is labelled `outage` or `heal` only when the fault journal (lines after the start reading's count) explains it, and on HPC the launch count and the state's recorded stops agree; any other restart is a `finding`. A read that cannot be made is `unknown` with its reason, never healthy, and every call only reads. `ComposeDriver.inspect_state` is the new Compose read (`observe` is unchanged). `docs/interfaces.md`, "The platform layer's read of the federation's own services".
- Every run's window on the federation's services, recorded by the platform itself (O86; `SandboxPlatform(..., federation_watch=)`, `expcore_onedata.sandbox.fedwindow`). `launch` calls `FederationWatch.read()` before the run's tokens and sandbox exist, so before any injection the run's body makes, and `window(start)` once the body has ended and before the stop, so after the restore of a fault injector whose `running` is inside the launch; the window is recorded as `federation_window` in the sandbox's `metadata()["infrastructure_facts"]` on both sandbox backends (every Docker run of a platform gains an `infrastructure_facts`, holding at least this one): the verdict, each service's status and restarts (`outage`, `heal` or `finding`), the findings and the unknowns, by label and never a name, address, node or secret. A run whose injection is in its window reads `restarted` with the restart labelled `outage`; a restart the journal does not explain is a finding. A read that fails is `unknown` with the exception's type and never fails the run, a body's error and a cancellation stand over it, and each read is inside a `sandbox_lifecycle` wait. A platform built without `federation_watch` reads nothing and records `unknown` ("no federation watch configured") instead of leaving the key absent, so a post-check sees the gap. `collect()` writes `metadata.json` before the restore, so the launch also writes the closed window as `federation-window.json` beside the `metadata.json` of each directory `collect` wrote into (a directory that is gone is skipped, one that cannot be written is logged, never a failure), and the `metadata.json` collect wrote holds a pending placeholder; read `metadata()` once the launch has ended. **Both executor orders are judged, and only the run's own injection explains anything (O86, #371's owner).** `FederationWatch.window(start, *, owner=None)` takes the run's owner token (the launch passes `liveness.owner()`, taken in its own context): only the injections whose `owner` names that run's attempt (`fault_owner.attempt_of`) explain a restart or hold a service down, so an earlier attempt's leftover and a line written before owners were recorded are a finding; with no `owner` (a batch's window) any injection counts, as before. A new status and verdict, **`held`**: a service not running at the window's end that an open injection the run owns holds down (the service was down at the start too, or its stop was asked in the window) is explained and no finding, and not `restarted`, as nothing restarted: it is the pilot's S3 order, injecting before the launch and restoring after it. Inside the launch the window still reads `restarted`, the restart labelled `outage`. The verdict follows the worst service: `finding`, then `unknown`, `held`, `restarted`, `stable`; only the injection's target may read held or restarted, and any other service that restarted or is down is a finding. The restore comes after the window, so `held` is read with the run's restore outcome (`instruments["fault_injector"]["restore_verified"]`, #367's verified step), which the facts point at (`restore`), and a held service whose restore failed is not a pass. The facts also carry `ownership` and each held service's `held`. Before this, a pilot-order run's target read a finding and a down service an open injection held read `restarted`. `docs/interfaces.md`, "The platform layer's read of the federation's own services".
- The HPC read of the federation's services waits, bounded, for Slurm's accounting to show a step the driver stopped (O86; `deploy/hpc-federation/hpcobserve.py`, `hpcslurm.accounting_behind`, `expcore_onedata.fedwatch.watch_for(..., accounting_wait_s=)`). The driver confirms a stop once `squeue -s` no longer lists the step and `sacct` follows seconds later, so in the first study's order (the target is stopped just before the window's start reading) every run would have read the target `unknown`. When the driver's state says the step was stopped (the service's `state` is `stopped`, its last recorded stop is confirmed, the step is the one of its `launch`) and `squeue` and `sacct` do not yet agree that it ended, the read polls every 2 s, every such service under one bound, until they agree; the seconds are recorded in the side (`accounting_wait_s_used`) and, as `start` and `end`, in each run's facts (`accounting_wait_s_used`). The bound is a declared setting of the watch, `accounting_wait_s`, 60 s unless declared, from 0 to 300; `hpcobserve.py --accounting-wait-s` takes it, and `HpcSource`'s command gets that long on top of `command_timeout_s`. Past it the service is `unknown` with the reason `accounting_lag`: never running, never stopped by assumption. It never waits for a service the state does not say was stopped, for a step squeue lists under another id than the state's, or for a command that failed, and everything stays read-only; the cross-check of the state's stops and launches is unchanged. `docs/interfaces.md`, "The platform layer's read of the federation's own services".
- A guard on the HPC account's home, read before `up` submits anything (O57; `deploy/hpc-federation/hpcquota.py`, `hpcsettings.py`, `hpcdriver.py`, `fleet.py`, their README section "The account's home, and its guard", DESIGN.md section 11 and `site.env.example`). The account's home is small and shared by every agent of the account, and a checkout or a venv left in it fills it fast. With `HPC_HOME_QUOTA_CMD` or `HPC_HOME_QUOTA_PATTERN` set in `site.env`, with or without a scratch root, `hpcfed.py up` reads the home's usage and limits through the declared command (split as a shell would split it, run without one, bounded at 120 s) and the pattern (the named groups `files_used`, `files_limit`, `bytes_used` and `bytes_limit`, exactly one match, each figure whole), and refuses when the used files or the used bytes pass 95% of their limit (`used > limit * 95 // 100`, in integers), naming each figure that is past, its limit and the line, with nothing submitted. It fails closed (a half-set check, an unreadable home, a limit of 0, which is what a pattern on a soft figure that reads 0 gives), and its messages hold figures only, never the tool's stderr or command line. `HPC_HOME_QUOTA_CHECK=off` skips it and `up` says so; with neither key set there is no check, as before, and a site should set them. `fleet.py up` and `add` read the home once for all their federations, ahead of the inode guard (`Hooks.home_guard`: a `Hooks` built by hand needs it). The three keys are not recorded in the state file.
- A REST arm for HPC federations, with the connect-side 443 mapper pinned like the agent's other files (#384, O63, O74; `deploy/hpc-federation/layouts/pilot-shape-rest.toml`, `hpcpins.py`, `hpcsettings.py`, `hpcidentity.py`). A REST level on an HPC federation needs a `[sandbox] content_check_file`, a REST level in `[sandbox.data_paths]` and `[sandbox.apptainer] preload` for `bundle/gateport.c`, which the pilot's platform template does not declare. The new layout is the pilot's but for its own template, which adds those three and nothing else (the pilot's is unchanged); the check file is the study space's `hello.txt`, which the layout's own baseline writes. The mapper and its SHA-256 are two optional `site.env` keys, `HPC_GATEPORT` and `HPC_GATEPORT_SHA256`, filled into `@hpc_gateport@` and `@hpc_gateport_sha@` and checked by `up` before it submits anything, as the egress shim is: a template that names them without the keys, a missing file and a file with another SHA-256 are refused, naming the variable and never the path. The render identity holds `gateport_sha256` when the template names the preload and no such key otherwise (never null), and the shard step's copy of the record's keys includes it.
- A study names the provider its content check reads through, and every run records the OS release, glibc and kernel of its node, or of the Docker host (O87). `[sandbox] content_check_provider = "<a provider's layout name>"` (optional, both backends, with `content_check_file`) has the pre-campaign content check read through that provider, the file's owner, instead of the agent's local provider, so a study whose states want every file empty at the local provider before a run no longer has the check fill it; unset, the check reads as before. A name that is no provider of the platform, or none that supports the check file's space, is refused at load naming the key. It is in `PlatformConfig.fingerprint()` (`sandbox.content_check_provider`, by position) only when it is not the local provider, so existing stores resume. Each run's `infrastructure_facts.node`, on Apptainer and on Docker (the host that runs the agent's container, the driver's own machine), holds the `ID` and `VERSION_ID` of `/etc/os-release` (read as text, never executed), the glibc version and the kernel release, in one shape under one name, and on Apptainer `tool_server_host` the same where the tool server's host differs from the node's; the HPC node facts step prints them and the driver's state keeps them per service. Recorded only: a reading that cannot be made is `unknown` with its reason and nothing is refused (`interfaces.md`, "The Apptainer backend on an HPC batch node").
- One way to read a run's infrastructure after its loop, on Docker and on an HPC node (O17, O18; `api.InfraReadings`). `sandbox.infra_check()` (the run's other components by role, their deaths and the infrastructure's problems), `sandbox.mount_errors()` (oneclient's own log summarised) and `sandbox.mount_log()` (that log's text, the token replaced by `<token>`) have the same names and shapes on both backends, so a study's post-run check needs no `docker exec`, `sandbox.host` or `sandbox.names` of its own. On Docker they are what `infra_container_check()` and `sidecar_errors()` read, byte for byte; those names still answer, on both backends, and warn (`DeprecationWarning`) naming the new ones. On the Apptainer backend the check reads the collector, the gate, the mount's oneclient with its mount and the mount guard's watcher by role (`apptainer.infra`), each judged, when the harness stopped it, by its state just before; **the collection's `deaths` (and `container_deaths` in the metadata) now come from it, so a mount run whose oneclient, mount or watcher ended during the run has that death recorded**, where only the gate and the collector were, and the metadata also holds `infra_check` and `infra_death`, as Docker's does. `mount_log()` may be read before the agent's first turn: the mount guard does not watch oneclient's log directory (O60).
- The run's tool server on an HPC node, placed by the sandbox (O56; `ApptainerSandbox.tool_server_process`, `api.ServerProcess`, `ToolServerLauncher.launch(..., process=)`). It was spawned there as on Docker, a child of the driver with the MCP client's variables of the driver's environment, in the driver's working directory and without the run's tag. Now its `HOME` and working directory are a 0700 directory of the run's (its `TMPDIR` in it), removed once it stops or, first, by a teardown that keeps the run as evidence; it carries the run's tag, so a dead driver's reaper kills it and the lease's probe holds the key while it lives; and its environment is named whole, `env -u` unsetting before its command each variable the client would pass on, so nothing of the driver's reaches it but `PATH` and no value is an argument. `extra_env` may not set its `HOME`, `TMPDIR` or tag. A Docker run's launch is unchanged.
- A health check can be told which attempt it judges (O82; `expcore.campaign.healthgate.CheckContext`, `HealthGate.before_run(run_key, attempt=...)`, `after_run(run_key, attempt=..., attempt_id=...)`). A check that declares a parameter named `context` is called `check(context=ctx)` with a frozen `CheckContext(run_key, attempt, phase, attempt_id)`: the run, the attempt's number (a pre-run check's is the number its try will have), the phase (`pre_run` or `post_run`) and, in a post-run check, the store's id of the attempt's row, which tells apart two attempts of one run that share a number; the dispatcher's own check, which judges no attempt, receives None. A study's owner-based check (a marker that charges only the attempt that left it) uses it in place of inspecting its callers' stacks (`docs/interfaces.md`, the health check). **Every other check is called with no argument as before**: only the name `context` opts in, so a check with `*args`, `**kwargs` or a parameter of another name with a default is unchanged. **One behaviour changes:** a check whose signature the gate could never fill (a required parameter of another name, or a `context` that cannot be passed by keyword) used to read as a failed check at every call and is now refused with a `ConfigError` when the scheduler is built. `ContextHealthCheck` is the protocol of a check that takes it.
- The oneclient that served a Compose mount run, in its record (O65; `sandbox.oneclient_build`). The Docker sandbox's mount record, `metadata()["mount"]`, has `oneclient_build` as the Apptainer host mount's has: `{"version", "fuse", "build_id", "image"}` (oneclient's `--version` words, the GNU build-id note of its binary, and the sidecar image's id in place of the bundle's SHA-256), read at the end of the mount's start by `docker exec` in the running sidecar, never `docker cp` (O05), and `null` for a reading that fails, which never refuses the start. **Additive:** an optional key under `mount`; a run on the REST path or with no data path is unchanged. `version_of` and `build_id` moved to `sandbox/oneclient_build.py` (re-exported by `apptainer.oneclient_bundle`); `build_id` now reads through any `(offset, size)` reader, asks for the program-header table and each note segment only up to a bound, and no longer reads a note cut short as a shorter id; the Apptainer start's refusal of a `--version` that names none says so without "on this node". Not run live: the reading through `docker exec` against the real sidecar image (`tests/live/test_mount_path.py` asserts it when the live gates are set).
- An HPC federation's provider storage on the cluster's shared scratch filesystem, bound onto each provider's posix storage, and the inode guard that keeps it within the account's quota (O57; `deploy/hpc-federation/hpcquota.py`, `hpcstorage.py`, `bundle/posixstorage.py`, README "Provider storage on scratch", `DESIGN.md` section 11). Four optional keys in `site.env`: `HPC_STORAGE_ROOT` (a directory on scratch; with none, nothing below runs, the providers store on their nodes' tmpfs, and `up` and `down` are what they were), `HPC_QUOTA_CMD` (the site's command that prints the account's inode usage and quota, run without a shell), `HPC_QUOTA_PATTERN` (a regular expression with the named groups `used` and `quota` that reads its output, exactly one match) and `HPC_QUOTA_CHECK=off` (refused with `HPC_STORAGE_ROOT`, as the site is loaded and again by the guard, since the check is mandatory for a federation bound onto scratch; without a scratch root there is no check to skip). With a scratch root `hpcfed.py up` reads the usage and the quota before it submits anything, estimates the federation's inodes from its data manifests (every space's manifest once, files and directories), and refuses when the usage plus the estimate would pass 95% of the quota, naming the used count, the estimate, the quota and the line; it fails closed, so an unset command or pattern, a command that fails, a pattern that does not match exactly once and a quota of 0 or `unlimited` all refuse `up`, naming why, and nothing turns the check off. It then makes the federation's own directory on scratch and one per provider, each by an exclusive `mkdir` that never adopts what is there, recorded in the state file first, and in each provider's, beside its owner marker, the empty directory it stores in. Each provider's job is given that directory (`--storage=PATH`, from the state's record checked whole), which the node checks (up's form for this provider, no link on the way, 0700 and this user's, the marker naming the provider and allocation, empty and off node-local memory at the first start) and binds writable onto `/volumes/posix` at every start, restarts included, each start naming the path again from the state (`hpcfed.py start` passes it) and binding only when the work directory's record names exactly it, so neither an edited record nor a lost one can redirect a provider or leave it on tmpfs; without the key the provider's step and the service's argv are word for word what they were. The cell sees its storage at the same path, so the storage is declared to Onedata as before and the data check, which reads each space through its owner, reads it where the owner stores it. Where a provider stores its data shapes a run's I/O timing, so the kind is declared and recorded: `[sandbox.apptainer] provider_storage` (`"tmpfs"` or `"shared_filesystem"`, one word, never the root's path), which `up` fills into the pilot's template from whether `HPC_STORAGE_ROOT` is set (`@hpc_provider_storage@`), is in `PlatformConfig.fingerprint()` (`sandbox_backend`) and in every run's `backend` and `infrastructure_facts`, so a tmpfs federation's shards and a scratch one's never merge; a template that declares the sandbox without it is refused by `up`, and the renderer reads its file back for it. A platform that declares none (a Compose federation, or one written before the key) keeps its fingerprint, and the fingerprint of an HPC federation's platform file differs from before this key. `down` removes exactly those, after the job is gone and every service is verified clean, never through a link and never anything it cannot prove `up` made, and counts what remains, failing loudly on any entry. The marker stays outside the bind, so the provider never sees it. The estimate counts an ingested space's declared manifest (`data_manifest`, which `up` loads, from `HPC_STUDY_ARTIFACTS`): the study's own data is in the guard, the pilot's largest space being 15,340 files, and an ingested space that declares none counts nothing. The live run of the bind and of the guard is open.
- A federation's render identity, and a shard step that runs only against the one its plan expects (O74; `deploy/hpc-federation/hpcidentity.py`, `shards.py`, its README). `hpcfed.py up` writes `render-identity.json` in the root (mode 0600, canonical JSON, sorted keys): `version`, the SHA-256 of the platform template before it was rendered, of the rendered platform's `PlatformConfig.fingerprint()` as the core hashes a fingerprint, and of each pin the platform holds (`sandbox_sif_sha256`, `egress_shim_sha256`, `egress_collector_sha256`, `oneclient_bundle_sha256`, the values `up` checked), and no address, id, node, path, user or secret. The shard manifest's optional `expect_render_identity` names the record a plan expects (a path of the study, the guard's path rules); `plan` and `lines` record its SHA-256 and each line ends in `--expect-render-identity REL --expect-render-identity-sha256 HEX`, and with none declared the lines and the plan are what they were. `step` compares the federation's record with it before anything is made, beside the pre-step guard and before it, and refuses with exit status 73 on any difference, naming the keys and no value, or when the record is missing, unreadable or malformed; and every step writes the record it validated into `out/study-state/render-identity.<step id>.json` at its start (a file of its own per step), so every snapshot, a killed step's `out/` and the archive carry it. With the optional `HPC_STUDY_ARTIFACTS` in `site.env` (the directory of the study's own artifacts: its tool server's clone and venv, its manifests), the record also holds `study_artifacts_tree_sha256`, the SHA-256 of that directory's tree (`hpcidentity.tree_sha256`), taken once in `up`'s preflight on the login node, before the root is made or a job submitted, kept in the state file and handed to the core's phase, so `[hpc] core = "step"` has it. It is the digest of one canonical listing, `expcore-artifacts-tree/1` and a NUL and then a record per entry sorted by its relative path's bytes (`F NUL path NUL size NUL sha256 NUL`, `D NUL path NUL`, `L NUL path NUL target NUL`), with no mode, owner, time or path of the directory, so a copy at another path has the same digest and the record holds no path. A link is recorded by its target string and never followed, and the directory is self-contained (its interpreter is installed in it, its venv built on it): a link whose resolved target leaves it is refused, as is a path through more than 40 links (a link is resolved as the kernel resolves it, against the tree's own entries alone and never opening anything outside; an absolute target must begin with the root's real path or the path it was declared by, compared by whole components; a link that dangles inside is recorded as before; the digest of a tree whose links stay inside is unchanged), a special file or a root that is no directory of its own is refused naming `HPC_STUDY_ARTIFACTS` and a relative path never an absolute one, and the tree is opened with no link followed and never written, so the pilot's read-only tree is read as any other. A federation with no such directory has no such key; `step` compares the key like any other and names it on a difference.
- A study's own refusals asserted at every HPC shard step's start, and its own check of the results before they are final (O63; `deploy/hpc-federation/shards.py`, its README). The shard manifest's `pre_step` names a script inside the study (a path relative to its root: no absolute path, no `..`, no link, a regular file of at most 1 MiB) and `pre_step_timeout_s` bounds it (60 s by default, at most 600); `post_run` and `post_run_timeout_s` (300 s, at most 1800) name a second one. `plan` and `lines` record each one's SHA-256 and each line ends in `--pre-step*` and `--post-run*` words; with none declared the lines and the plan are what they were. The step reads and hashes both before anything is made, then runs the guard first, before the core check, a resume's removal of a killed step's directory, the sandbox's work root, its own directory, the journals or an archive: the bytes it hashed, as `python -I -B - LABEL CAMPAIGN.TOML`, in the stamped study's root, which it holds open and refuses unless it is this user's and no group or other can write to it, under the step's allowlist and no-home variables, in a session of its own, killed with everything it started at the bound or when the step is told to stop. A non-zero exit, a signal, a timeout, a hash that is not the plan's or an unreadable guard refuses the step with exit status 73, naming the guard, and leaves nothing made. The post-run script runs after the dispatcher exits 0 and before the archive, while the federation is up (`python -I -B - LABEL OUT/ STATUS`, in the study copy's root, in the dispatcher's environment); a pass makes the shard's results final, and anything else, or a dispatcher that did not exit 0, archives them all the same with `final: false` in the manifest and `NOT-FINAL.json` in the archive's `out/` (written into the archive itself), and the step ends with 74. Each script's record (path, hash, whether it ran, exit status) is in the step's record and the archive's manifest; `collect` refuses a shard that is not final, or, with a post-run script in the plan, one that did not run it to a pass, and the core's `merge` refuses an `out/` holding `NOT-FINAL.json`. A line run directly runs both scripts; `--archive-left` starts no work, runs neither, and archives final only what its step's record says passed.
- The data of a space the study's own ingest fills, declared and verified on an HPC federation, and a placeholder for the study's own paths (C06, O57, O63; `deploy/hpc-federation`). A space that is neither a baseline space nor a shared import (`baseline = false`, its `@data_manifest.<space>@` named by the platform template) holds nothing at `up`, so `up` filled its manifest from the empty baseline and two federations that ingested different bytes fingerprinted alike. Such a space now declares its manifest, `data_manifest` on the space in the layout (absolute, or under `@study_artifacts@`) in the core's import-manifest format (`expcore_onedata.importmanifest`; `Manifest.digest()` is the hash `PlatformConfig.fingerprint()` holds), and `up` renders that path and loads it before any job is held (and refuses a rendered file whose entry for a space names a `data_manifest` of the template's own, which nothing would check); one that declares none is held to the empty manifest. `up` checks no ingested space (it holds nothing yet) and records each in the state file; `hpcfed.py verify-data` reads each back through its owner once the ingest is done, compares every file's path, size and SHA-256 and every directory with the manifest, exits 1 on any difference naming the space and the counts, and records a marker bound to the manifest's digest; `shards.py plan` and `lines` refuse a federation whose ingested spaces have no marker (or whose marker is for another manifest, or whose state has no record of them). A baseline space and a shared import keep their check at `up`. A platform template, or a space's `data_manifest`, may name `@study_artifacts@`, which `up` fills from the new optional `site.env` key `HPC_STUDY_ARTIFACTS` (an absolute path on a filesystem the login node and the agent's node see; refused, naming it, when a template uses the placeholder without it), so a public replication package carries no site path. **Changed:** a federation brought up by an earlier driver has no record of its ingested spaces, and `plan` refuses it (down and up again); `hpcpins.values` leaves out a site value that is unset, so the renderer refuses a placeholder nothing filled.
- A shard killed outright resumed on another node, checked (O63; `deploy/hpc-federation/shards.py resume-line`, `shardresume.py`). `shards.py resume-line --manifest M --label L --job J --node N` prints the shard's step line with `--resume` in a held job of the operator's only once J is RUNNING, theirs, of one component and the one node N, no federation's services' job, with the manifest's cores and memory free; no step of the shard is listed under any of their jobs and its running mark names no step still listed; and the newest snapshot that verifies names the shard and its archive and pins the campaign fingerprint the study pins with this federation's `platform.toml` (`expcore.study.pipeline.campaign_fingerprint`, new), as every other shard does. It records J and N in the federation's state (`shard_nodes`), and `down` sweeps that node in that job, never cancelling it. Each shard step keeps a running mark, `<results>/<label>/running.json`, from its start to its own end: a start is refused while one is there, and a `--resume` takes one over only on its own node once its process is gone, or with the nonce `resume-line` showed gone (`--over`), by that mark's one claim, made exclusively. `down` cancels a shard step of the federation still running in the operator's job before it sweeps that node. Live slot 5 resumed one by a hand-edited line nothing checked.
- Slots and a tool server on both platforms, and a slot user that exists is never changed (O83; `deploy/compose-federation/fedtools.py`, `fedboot.py`, `deploy/hpc-federation/layouts/pilot-shape-slots.toml`). A slot's users are ordinary principals of the layout, so the bootstrap that makes principals makes them (their passwords 0600 files in the root, on HPC the storage mapping too), and a platform template declares `[slots]` and `[toolserver]` with `@user_id.<name>@` and `@study_artifacts@`. A slot user that already exists is verified and recorded `matched` with nothing sent, or refused naming the user and the attribute (rights, membership, another space, a group, a cluster, the password), never changed. The rendered file is loaded as a campaign loads it and checked against the layout's principals (rights typed in a template must be the principal's privileges); `up` checks the tool server's files before anything is brought up. HPC reads the directory from `HPC_STUDY_ARTIFACTS` in `site.env`, Compose from `EXPCORE_FED_STUDY_ARTIFACTS`. A layout with no slot renders what it always did. Owed to a live slot: a B2 run whose tool calls succeed, whose rights refusals match Compose's and whose visible-names counts are zero.
- Every Onedata service of an HPC federation registers and is reached by the name its layout declares (O73; `expcore_onedata/addresses.py`, `deploy/hpc-federation/hpcnames.py`, `bundle/configure.sh`). `up` writes a private address map into the root, from each declared name to its node's address and the node's own names, and `platform.toml` names it (`[addresses] file`). The platform layer's client, the bootstrap's, the gate (`dial` beside `addr`), the host mount's oneclient and the day-0 reachability check dial the mapped address while the URL, the TLS handshake, the certificate check and `Host` carry the declared name. The services' URLs, `facts.json` and `platform.toml` hold the declared names, and each service registers under its name (`onezone.domainName`, `oneprovider.domain`, the generated certificate's domain), with a hosts file of ours bound over `/etc/hosts` that lists every service's declared name. `facts.json` records the domain each service registered (read back and checked), and Onepanel's DNS check as advisory. The tool server is given no `ONEDATA_MCP_NODE_NAMES` (once the services register the declared names there is nothing to map, so onedata-mcp's text is the same on both platforms); a Python server is started through a launcher of the root's (`deploy/hpc-federation/toolshim/resolve_launch.py`, named by `[addresses] resolver`): `python -I -B resolve_launch.py <map> -- -m <module> ...` wraps `socket.getaddrinfo` so that exactly the names of the private map resolve and every other host as before, then runs the server (no `PYTHONPATH`, no `sitecustomize`, nothing in its environment; Python servers only). The data gate also counts, in memory, the exact node names and addresses of the address map's real side in each answer it relays (never a body, a matched name or its context), and a run's `infrastructure_facts.rest_node_name_hits` carries the count (`version`, `rest_answers`; unknown, never zero, when the gate's log is not shown whole; absent without names). A `[sandbox.federation]` table whose names are not the registered ones is refused. The live checks are still owed to a sandbox slot (see O73).

- The gate's log gives every request it relayed its duration (`duration_ms`, to the last byte relayed or to the failure) and, of the upstream attempt that waited longest, the wait for the first answer byte (`upstream_wait_ms`), the connection it was on (`upstream_wait_conn`, `new` or `reused`) and how it ended (`upstream_wait_end`), so a stall on a reused keep-alive connection is measurable even when the request was retried on a new one and answered; a request still in flight when the gate stops has its age on its connection's teardown line (`request_age_ms`). A run's `infrastructure_facts.rest_request_durations` sums them once the gate is stopped and its log whole: `version`, `requests`, per connection `requests`, `over_10s`, `over_30s` and `over_60s`, and `max_duration_ms` (unknown, never zero, when the log is not shown whole). No body, header value or name is logged, and the gate's timeouts, retry and connection reuse are unchanged (O73).
- The layout's names for an HPC agent's REST cell (O69; `apptainer/gaterun.hosts`, `sandbox.settings`). On the Apptainer backend `[sandbox.federation]` takes `zone` and `providers.<local>` (no `network`, a rest level required, two different names), and the gate serves and certifies them on the cell's loopback, on 443, dialling the URLs' hosts and ports behind them as Docker's gate does on its network. The cell's `ONEDATA_ONEZONE_HOST`, `/etc/hosts`, `gate.json`, the gate's certificates and the egress declaration then hold the declared names, never a node's FQDN or 8443, and `metadata()["gate"]["names"]` says `declared` or `urls`. The gate's 502 for a failed upstream call names the dialled host by its served name (`gate._as_served`). Without the table nothing changes. The providers' registered domains, which the zone's scope answer carries and the published client dials, are not changed here, so the table is usable only on a federation whose local provider registered its declared name.
- Test selections on a standing HPC allocation (O64; `deploy/hpc-tests`, its README). `hpctests.py run --repo <worktree> -- <pytest arguments>` ships the commit (its history as a git bundle) into one `srun --exact` step of an allocation of ours, behind an environment allowlist; the node clones it on its tmpfs, builds its environment with `uv sync --frozen`, checks that an isolated interpreter imports the core from the clone (exit 97 otherwise) and runs pytest; the results (exit status, summary, `-ra` lines, JUnit XML) come back as one archive checked by SHA-256. `alloc.sh` on the login node submits the allocation with a verified read-back, lists and releases only jobs its ledger holds under our name, and renews it from the clock with a `--begin` successor while a test step of ours ran within 3 h (`renew start --until`; `renew want --until` and `renew never` force either way), and releases an allocation of ours that has run no test step for 3 h. Site values come from a local `site.env`, never the repository.
- The host mount's oneclient on HPC batch nodes: a relocatable bundle of the Compose sidecar's pinned image, declared and fingerprinted (O65; `deploy/hpc-federation/oneclient_bundle.py`, `apptainer.oneclient_bundle`). `oneclient_bundle.py --image <ref> --id sha256:<id> --out <dir> [--self-check]` builds, through one never-started scratch container, a reproducible tar of the binary, its library closure resolved inside the image, the NSS modules glibc opens by name and the image's own loader, with its `.sha256` and provenance. **Changed:** a mount level on the Apptainer backend needs `[sandbox.apptainer] oneclient_bundle` and `oneclient_bundle_sha256` (the pilot's template fills them from `site.env`'s `HPC_ONECLIENT_BUNDLE` and its `_SHA256`, which `up` checks), whose SHA-256 enters `PlatformConfig.fingerprint()`'s `sandbox_backend` (a platform without a bundle keeps its fingerprint); `host_mount_factory()` takes no oneclient any more and raises `TypeError` on one, since its runs would carry no pin. Each mount run unpacks its own copy from the bytes it hashes, runs oneclient as `<loader> --inhibit-cache --inhibit-rpath <the bundle's files> --library-path <lib>`, so neither the image's RPATH nor the node's loader cache is consulted, and records `oneclient_build` (`version`, `fuse`, `build_id`, `bundle_sha256`) in the mount helper's metadata (the sandbox's `metadata()["mount"]`); the build id is the binary's GNU build-id note, since `oneclient --version` prints none. Shown live on one batch node: the unpack, `--version` and a `--help` dry run, nothing outside the bundle tried or opened at `--version`; the mount itself is not yet run live.
- A space's data identity in the campaign fingerprint, in place of its Onezone id (C06). A space is the same experimental condition across federations when its declared identity and its data identity match: an entry of `[[spaces]]` may declare `data_manifest`, the manifest of the data it holds, and is then fingerprinted by its declared identity and that manifest's content hash, without the Onezone id every federation's `up` makes anew, so the shards of two federations holding the same data merge and one file different refuses them. An entry without it keeps its id-bearing digest, unchanged. A plugin may give `StudyPlugin.platform_legacy` (`PlatformConfig.legacy_fingerprint()`), so a store recorded before its spaces declared their data resumes under its own record; the merge never takes such a store beside one of the later form.
- An Apptainer sandbox backend for HPC batch nodes (#179, O56; `expcore_onedata.sandbox.apptainer`). `[sandbox] backend = "apptainer"` with `[sandbox.apptainer]` (the agent's SIF, the bundle's egress shim and collector, each pinned by its SHA-256) makes `SandboxPlatform` run `ApptainerSandbox` and its lease probe on a node where an unprivileged user gets no namespace but setuid Apptainer's and no cgroup per container: every cell one `apptainer exec --containall` under the completion bootstrap (O55), the core's gate a process of the driver's on 127.0.0.1 holding the token in its memory alone, the egress collector started first and proved by a canary, a harness-side watch keeping the memory, `/work`, `/tmp` and `/dev/shm` bounds, and an ordered, verified teardown with run records a later start reaps and the probe reads. Every departure from the Docker sandbox is in each run's `infrastructure_facts`. A mount run is refused at its start unless the sandbox is given the mount's helper (hook c, O60) and the platform declares `[sandbox.mount]`; the gate's options (hook a) and the cell's preloaded libraries and binds (hook b) are declared there too. `killcause` gains `disk_limit`, the agent's own; `hostops.run_bounded` gains `inherit_env`. The Docker backend's behaviour and fingerprint are unchanged. `deploy/hpc-federation/shards.py` runs such a campaign as one #154 shard per HPC federation: each dispatcher a step on its federation's sandbox node, its `out/` archived whole after it exits, and every shard verified and unpacked for `merge`.
- The HPC path rehearsed end to end offline, and the seams it found closed (O63; `tests/e2e/test_hpc_rehearsal.py`). Two federations brought up by `hpcfed.py up` over fake clusters, two shards of a stub-model study run by `shards.py` as their nodes would run them, every run in the Apptainer sandbox on a fake node, a shard killed outright after a snapshot of its third attempt in flight, and resumed, the merge, `check`, `analyze`, `report`, and `down`, each stage reading only the files the one before it wrote. What it closed: the pilot's platform template declares the agent's Apptainer sandbox, with placeholders `up` fills from `site.env` (`HPC_AGENT_SIF`, `HPC_AGENT_SIF_SHA256`, `HPC_EGRESS_SHIM`, `HPC_EGRESS_SHIM_SHA256`, `HPC_AGENT_WORK_ROOT`; the collector is the bundle's own), each declared file checked against its SHA-256 before the cluster is touched and named by its variable when it fails, so `pilot-shape.toml` holds its agent's node; every path `up` renders into the root is absolute (`@hpc_root@`); `shards.py step` makes the sandbox's `work_root` (0700, under `--tmpfs` only) and copies its federation's `platform.toml` beside the study's `study.toml`, refusing a study that holds one; a step's directory on the node's tmpfs records its process, `--resume` and `down` (a control step on the agent's node, `shards.py sweep`) remove those whose step is shown gone, and one whose `out/` could not be archived is marked, kept, named, refused by `--resume` and archived by `step --archive-left`. Each template's spaces declare their data (`data_manifest`): a shared import's own manifest, every other space's baseline manifest written by `up`, and `up` reads every space back and fails, naming it, when its data is not its manifest; `shards.py plan` refuses a campaign on several federations with a space's data undeclared, and a federation whose `up` did not finish. The one-space form (`[space]`) takes `data_manifest` too, its data identity then in the fingerprint (`space_data`), unchanged without it (C06).
- The shard archives' copy back to the operator's host, made checkable, with the evidence that lies beside them (O63; `shards.py collect`, `shards.py verify-copy`, the HPC README and DESIGN.md). Nothing durable may live only on a cluster's scratch, since a site's scratch policy can remove files 30 days after creation, a job directory 7 days after its job and all of scratch after 20 days with no job submitted, and the cluster cannot push to the operator's host, so the operator pulls the results directory (rsync or scp, from their host) after `collect` and before `down`, and checks it. `collect` writes `archives.sha256.json` beside the archives (`expcore-hpc-archives/1`: a `plan_id`, the SHA-256 of the plan's study and results directories and each shard's index, count, label and root, and each archive's relative name, size and SHA-256, taken from the private copy it verified and never from a step's own claim; then `evidence`, what lies beside the archives and would go with the scratch: each shard's `<label>.out.json`, every file under its `<label>/journals/` (the spend journals: what a step killed outright spent, which `--resume` counts as `lost_in_kill`), and, only when it is newer than the archive (a step resumed from the archive was killed before it archived: the snapshot `--resume` picks, with a warning from `collect`, and `collect --run` refuses before any merge, naming the shard and the snapshot, since the merge would leave those runs out and has no flag to leave a shard short), its newest snapshot that verifies, each by relative name, size and SHA-256 of the bytes read; the same bytes for every collect of results that have not changed, and none from a refused collect) and prints that file's SHA-256 on stderr after the merge line, which stays alone on stdout. `shards.py verify-copy MANIFEST LOCAL_DIR --manifest-sha256 HASH` uses the standard library alone, so it runs on a host without the core, and checks that the manifest is the file whose hash `collect` printed, then that every archive and every evidence file it names is present, as a regular file under plain directories, with its size and SHA-256: exit 1 naming each missing or differing file (names and reasons, never a path), 2 for a manifest it cannot use (not the printed hash, no archive named, no evidence list, which is an earlier tip's manifest, a name that is not a plain file name or relative path), 0 otherwise, with a warning for an archive, archive manifest or journal in the directory that the manifest does not name. Snapshots that are not newer than the archive are resume material, superseded by the verified archive, and not part of the required pull (the HPC README says what is). *Not run live:* a pull from a real cluster and a verification on a real operator host.
- A fleet of HPC federations, one shard of a campaign on each (O68; `deploy/hpc-federation/fleet.py`, its README section and DESIGN.md, section 1, "A fleet"). `fleet.py --fleet DIR --layout L up N` brings N federations up from one layout, each its own root (`DIR/fed-k/root`, an exclusive `mkdir`), state file and held jobs (the agent's node a job of its own: a `het` layout is refused, since steps on a heterogeneous job's services failed live), each by `hpcfed.py up` in a process of its own (`-I`, from the fleet's 0700 directory), `HPC_FLEET_PARALLEL` at a time; it refuses past `HPC_FLEET_MAX`, the operator's maximum (unset refuses), and, with a scratch root (`HPC_STORAGE_ROOT`), past the quota for all N at once by the core's own inode guard (`hpcquota.guard`, O57: mandatory, so an override, an unset command or an unreadable quota refuses the fleet), before anything is made. `layouts/pilot-shape-separate.toml` is the pilot's layout with the agent's node held as a job of its own, the placement a fleet requires, and nothing else different. A failed `up` leaves the others up and is reported with the job it may hold. `plan` gives each shard a linked federation of the fleet's whose spaces declare their data, writes `DIR/shards.toml` for `shards.py collect`, and refuses outright when the federations' `PlatformConfig.fingerprint()` differ; `step` launches each shard's `shards.py` line on its own federation, checked again first, only from the manifest the record gives and only into a job Slurm still holds, running, under its federation's name, and exits 1 when it refuses one; `status` reads; `down` takes down every federation the fleet made, each verified, and refuses (untouched) a root that is not its `mkdir` or whose state is another federation's, its agent's job record included: the record (`fleet.json`) is the only list, and nothing is found by a search. A shard freed by `down --member` moves only to a federation brought up after, with the fleet's fingerprint and `[placement] reset_shortcut` off in its `platform.toml`, so its resets there take the full reads; its step resumes. `shards.py`'s step line now carries `--chdir=/` and its merge line `-I` (the F2 rule: nothing starts in, or imports from, the directory it was run from). Not run live.
- Snapshots of a running shard, and the spend a kill takes out of a store counted once (O62). A shard step on an HPC node keeps its store on the node's tmpfs, so a step killed outright lost its whole `out/`. `deploy/hpc-federation/shards.py step` now snapshots it into `<results>/<label>/snapshots/` every `snapshot_every_s` (900 s, a manifest key) and after each run's end (`expcore.campaign.out_snapshot`): the store first, by the online backup, then the rest of `out/` (`runs/` without the directories no row names, the lease notes, the logs), so the store never names a record `runs/` lacks; each written into a fresh directory, checked (integrity, every ended attempt's marker, every file read back) and renamed into place, the newest two kept. `--resume` restores the newest that verifies unless the archive is newer (and refuses a shard none of whose snapshots verifies that has no archive), and points the store's attempt directories, a snapshot's or an archive's, at the new copy's `runs/` (`snapshot.rebase_run_dirs`); a restored lease directory is marked rolled back (`lease.mark_rolled_back`), so every key needs its resource shown gone before a new holder (C19). `expcore run --spend-journal FILE` journals every attempt begun, top-up, attempt ended, probe charge and model taken off the reply maximum to a file on a filesystem that outlives the host, after the store commits it and before the spend, stopping the dispatcher when a line cannot be written (`expcore.campaign.spend_journal`); the next start counts what the journals hold and the store lacks once, as `lost_in_kill` in the store's meta (`expcore.campaign.lost_spend`, `lost_ledger`), charged by the budget gate and `spend_of` against the shard's share, listed in the export's optional `lost_in_kill` and the report's "Spend lost in kills". A journal or snapshot of another campaign or shard (by its whole spec) is refused, naming it. Without `--spend-journal` a campaign is what it was. Not run live.
- A study may set the agent's whole-time ceiling (O06): `[agent] ceiling_s` (seconds) or `ceiling_factor` (a multiple of `time_cap_s`, at least 1) in `campaign.toml`, at most one of them and never below the cap; without either it is still 3 times the cap, so no existing study changes. Until now the key was refused as unknown. The ceiling bounds a run's elapsed time, waits on infrastructure included, and ends it as infrastructure of kind `ceiling`; the token lifetime of the Onedata layer follows it, as before (the ceiling plus 1,200 s at the defaults of `[tokens]`), so a lower ceiling shortens how long a slot is held when tokens are not revoked at the end of a run. The ceiling is in the campaign's caps, so in the fingerprint: changing it needs a new store, and the same ceiling written as seconds and as a factor are two fingerprints. `SlotTokens.mint` writes `ttl_s` and `ceiling_s` on every line it adds to the lease journal (`TokenJournal.record` and `TokenJournal.record_indeterminate` take them; nothing reads them back), the sandbox's metadata records them with each token's `slot` and `expires_at`, and `SandboxPlatform.tool_server` takes `ceiling_s` as optional, defaulting to the ceiling its token was minted for and refusing another with `ValueError` before anything starts. All of this is in the platform's own launch, which the Docker and the Apptainer backends share, so a run on an HPC batch node mints, records and serves its tokens for its ceiling as a Docker run does. The study guide has a section, "Caps on a run's time", on what the cap and the ceiling count, the token window, the slot hold, planning a calendar and when to lower the ceiling.
- The optional hooks of an instrument are one exported list, and a wrapper can be checked against it (C31). `expcore.records.instruments.OPTIONAL_HOOKS` is the tuple of names the core looks for on an instrument (`at_first_turn`, `check`), and the core's discovery uses exactly it: it refuses to look up any other name, and a test compares the list with every name the module looks an instrument up by, so a hook added without the list fails it. A study's class that wraps an instrument and does not pass a hook on drops it without a word, so the wrapped instrument's refusal is never asked for; `missing_hooks(wrapper, wrapped)` returns the hooks the wrapped instrument has and the wrapper does not, in the list's order, for the study's own test (an attribute counts when it is callable, as the core counts it, so a property that answers None is no hook). The wrapper rule is in `docs/interfaces.md` ([a class that wraps an instrument](docs/interfaces.md#a-class-that-wraps-an-instrument)) and the study guide. Nothing a study already runs changes.
- A shortcut for the reset's occupancy and quiet stages, declared, and the federation's reset lock it rests on (O11, O46, O51). Both stages read every file's block map at every provider that can hold the space's bytes, about 106 s a pass for the first study's large space. With `[placement] reset_shortcut = true` (refused without `[faults]`) and a planner's reading made inside `SpaceReset.exclusive()` (or `SpacesReset.exclusive()`), they re-read only the files the reading showed held, those a transfer touched since it (the ended listings walked to the reading's start, and the jobs the transfers stage cancelled), the files the reset evicted, every file a QoS requirement applies to and every hard link, and carry the rest as empty (`files_carried`); the record's `occupancy_reads` gives the mode, why, the wait and the reading's time and size. The premise is that no reader places bytes meanwhile, and an on-demand read leaves no transfer record, so every shared admission (a campaign run's, a tool's outside a campaign, the health check's) now holds a new lock, `federation.reset`, shared beside `federation.lock`, recorded in `reset-holds.jsonl`, and the section takes it exclusively: the run gives up its own share of it, never of `federation.lock`, so no outage run can begin, and waits for it (every reset now does, and refuses at `[faults] admission_timeout_s`: O66, under "Fixed"); whenever the premise is in doubt (a holder that died, a reading outside a live section, a job since the reading on a directory, a listing that cannot be read), the reset does the full reads under the hold and never fails for it. While the section holds the lock, other admissions wait (uncharged), health checks are deferred (uncharged) and the run's own infrastructure is refused. Every lock file is now opened `O_CLOEXEC`; a shared admission whose hold of the reset lock cannot be recorded is refused. Default off: a study that declares nothing reads every file as before.
- A ratio contrast may give each arm a column of its own (S13), for a preregistered adverse sensitivity estimate that puts arm A at its upper-bound tokens and arm B at its lower-bound ones as a declared contrast beside the main one. `numerator_a` and `numerator_b` (and `denominator_a` and `denominator_b`) in `[contrasts.<name>]` each default to `numerator` (or `denominator`); a shared key beside both per-arm keys of its role, which no arm would read, is refused, and so are one per-arm key with no shared key, an arm whose numerator and denominator would be one column, and a per-arm key beside an estimator of one outcome. Each run is read in its own arm's columns, found from its condition or level (`pairing._columns`), so a cell's value is ln((sum X_A / sum D_A) / (sum Y_B / sum D_B)), a run of arm B need not have arm A's column, and a failed run whose numerator is None in its own arm's column refuses a confirmatory contrast (`RatioNumeratorRefused`, naming each arm's columns), while a None in the other arm's column does not. `RatioForm` gains the four fields, `columns(arm)` and `per_arm`, and is held one way however it is written (a role whose arms differ holds both arms' columns and `numerator` None); its `label`, the contrast's outcome, is `"tokens_high/success vs tokens_low/success"` when the arms differ. Additive in `analysis.json`: the per-arm fields are written only when set (a dataclass field marked `OMIT_NONE` is left out while None), so an existing ratio contrast's record is unchanged byte for byte; the report's Numerator and Denominator cells name each arm's column with its arm, with a note only when such a contrast is there.
- A tool outside a campaign holds the federation's shared admission (#266, O48). A study's smoke driver, warm-up or `verify` is no campaign attempt and held no admission, so it could read and write through a provider while an outage took it down or restored it. `expcore_onedata.tool_admission.shared_admission(config, reason=...)` (`shared_admission_async` for an event loop) takes the lock a campaign attempt takes, shared, through the same turnstile, so an outage run never overlaps it: it waits for one in progress, and for an outage run's session drain, within a bound (`timeout_s`, by default `[faults] admission_timeout_s`, 0 to try once), past which it raises `FederationBusy`, the uncharged hold `federation_busy` a campaign run's wait ends in, and refuses, naming why, while an injection is open or the fault journal cannot be read and, for the `ingest_journals` it is given, while a preallocated baseline is not whole or is held open by a live run. It records the reason, the process (boot id, pid and start time), the start and the end in `shared-holds.jsonl` beside the fault journal, releases on an exception, and a dead tool's record is ended as `holder_gone` by the next hold or any exclusive admission. An exclusive wait that runs out says that tools hold the federation and since when. The hold covers the `with` body: a worker that must outlive it takes `retain_admission()`. The study guide shows it for smoke drivers and warm-ups.
- A public way to run a study built in code (C37): `expcore.study.pipeline.run_built_study(study: StudyConfig, *, allow_dirty, code_root, crash_hook, shard)`, for a smoke or a selection cut from a loaded study with `dataclasses.replace`. A study that had no directory to name called the private `_run_campaign` and so skipped the scope of its own infrastructure kinds, and an `InfraError` of a kind it declares in `[study.kinds]` was refused as unknown in the executor's thread. `run_built_study` holds the study's kinds in scope for the whole run, and first holds the config to what `load_study` holds a study to (`expcore.study.config.check_study`, public too): each declared kind, the factor schema built again from its own parts (`FactorSchema.rebuilt`, so a treatment naming a kind the config lacks, or a kind that is a fault on one target without its target, is refused even in a schema edited after it was built, and so is a schema with any part edited, swapped or deleted after it was built, which the rebuild compares attribute by attribute and by digest), the `[scheduler]` kinds, the campaign's models against the model table, each contrast's cluster against the tasks, the shard budget and the expansion of the plan (a cut that leaves a condition naming a dropped task is refused). A `ConfigError` names the key and comes before any store, lease or code identity is touched. `run_study` loads and calls it, so there is one path, and `_run_campaign` is documented as internal. A cut's fingerprint is a different experiment from the whole campaign's, so run a smoke from a copy of the study directory with `code_root` the real one ([the guide](docs/study-guide.md#a-smoke-built-in-code)).
- The run's record carries the mount's declared and effective I/O mode (#247, O49). `expcore_onedata.sandbox.iomode.MountIoMode(sandbox)`, the platform's fourth instrument (namespace `mount_io_mode`), records `declared` (`proxy` or `direct`, the setting), `effective` (from oneclient's own start lines `Forced direct IO` and `Forced proxy IO`, and the lines of a detection or a fallback to the provider: `proxy`, `direct`, `auto` for no forced mode, `mixed` for a log that contradicts itself, `unknown` for a log that cannot be read) and `agree`, true only when the two are the same mode. The sandbox's collection reads oneclient's log once (`DockerSandbox.mount_io_mode`, kept in the metadata as `mount.effective`). **A run whose modes disagree, or whose log cannot say, is refused:** the instrument's check raises `InfraError("io_mode_integrity")`, a kind no condition can declare, so `finish_instruments` writes an `instrument_refusal` naming both modes and the attempt is set aside as infrastructure and run again, never scored as the declared mode; `MountIoMode(sandbox, refuse=False)` only records, and `agrees(snapshot)` recomputes the answer. A run with no mount records `{"version": 1, "mount": false}`. Additive: no record schema field changes and the fixtures of version 3 replay as before. Not yet run live (see O49 in `docs/regressions.md`).
- The export carries the campaign fingerprint and its hash (#243, C36). `export.json` has a last top-level key, `campaign_fingerprint`: `{"sha256", "fingerprint"}`, the fingerprint exactly as the store or its snapshot records it, and the SHA-256 of its canonical JSON, which is the `fingerprint` merge.json names. A report reads a setting that holds for the whole campaign (a mount level's declared `io_mode`, `tool_call_bound_s`) from the export, without opening `state.sqlite`, and checks which campaign it reads: `expcore.campaign.export.verify_export_fingerprint(export, stored_fingerprint_sha256(store_or_snapshot))` refuses an export of another campaign, naming both hashes, and says `fingerprint not recorded` of an export from before the key, which still reads. The key is additive and not required, so a reader that ignores it is unaffected; the fingerprint holds digests, never a path, a host or a secret (O12). A merged batch's export holds the fingerprint every shard shares, once. `analyze` checks the export it builds against every snapshot it read (and merge.json's hash), `package-check` names a saved export of another campaign, and an anonymised package's export holds the fingerprint without the environment's `sources`, as its snapshot does. `check` does not read `export.json`. See [`docs/interfaces.md`](docs/interfaces.md).
- The mount data path on HPC batch nodes, guarded and reported (#179, O60; `apptainer.hostmount`, `sandbox.mountwatch`, `expcore.mountguard`). The Apptainer backend's mount helper (hook c, `host_mount_factory(oneclient_pin)`) runs oneclient on the node outside every cell, its token in a 0600 file of a 0700 directory no cell binds, removed once the mount is up, and binds only the mount point into each cell. A watcher outside the cells logs every inotify event on the token file and its directory, samples oneclient for a tracer, and proves from the node's `/proc` that each cell it finds runs in a PID namespace of its own with no host `/proc` and no private directory in its mounts. The instrument `MountGuard` (namespace `mount_guard`) records the log's state, the accesses, the tracers and the isolation; a run is `clean`, `flagged` (an access) or `unknown` (a missing or damaged log, or an unproven isolation, never clean). Once one analysed record carries it, `analyze` judges every run, runs the preregistered analysis again without the runs that are not clean, and the report (`## Mount isolation (detected, not prevented)`), `analysis.json` (`mount_guard`) and the terminal show both, as for egress; a batch without it is unchanged. A batch checked by both guards also gets the rerun without every run either leaves out (`## Egress and mount isolation together`, `analysis.json` `guards`, `expcore.guards`). The sandbox gains `mount_watch_log()` and `mount_guard_nonce`, a mount run's collection copies `mount-watch.jsonl`, and the hook gains `token_paths()`, which the sandbox lists in the run's record before the token file is written, so a reaper removes it first. Record fixture `08-mount-guard.json`. Not run live yet.
- A run with no mount is not applicable for the mount guard (O75). In a campaign that mixes data paths the mount guard was listed on the mount runs alone, so every REST run read `unknown` and was left out of the rerun wholesale. `expcore.mountguard.mount_guard_for(sandbox)` builds the guard for a mount run and `NoMount` for a REST run or a run with no data path, which records `{"version": 1, "mount": false, "data_path": "rest" | "none"}`, so a study lists it for every run; `ApptainerSandbox.data_path_kind` is the spec's mode, so a mount run whose helper keeps no watcher is refused, never read as not applicable. `judge` reads exactly that statement as the verdict `not_applicable`: no flag, kept in the rerun, counted apart from `clean`, and shown in the report, `analysis.json` (`verdict`, `data_path`, counts) and the terminal; a mount run without its snapshot is still `unknown` and left out, and a statement of any other shape is `unknown`. The combined rerun leaves a run out only for a guard that applies to it, and any state it does not know leaves a run out. The egress and process-visibility checks apply to every run of an Apptainer campaign, so they have no such verdict. A record with no snapshot at all, as when the guard was listed on the mount runs alone, is not applicable when the campaign fingerprint's `data_paths` (`expcore.mountguard.campaign`) maps the run's level of the data-path factor to `rest` or `none`, **a factor a study names once, in `[study] data_path_factor` of `study.toml` (a declared factor, else refused at load; `data_path` unless set, and a study that sets no key is exactly as it was, its fingerprint included), read as `StudyConfig.data_path_factor` by the mount guard's judge and by `SandboxPlatform(..., data_path_factor=)` for a run's mode, so a study that calls the factor `path` renames nothing, and a campaign whose platform reads another name than the study declares is refused at its start, before any run, naming both (`StudyPlugin.data_path_factor`, `expcore.study.plugin.check_data_path_factor`)**, so no record has to be rewritten; a mount level, an unmapped level or no recorded data paths leave it `unknown`, and `analysis.json` says where each run's data path came from (`data_path_from`).
- The rerun of the guards together covers every guard that checked the batch (O76). `expcore.guards.guards_analysis(rows, absent, prereg, egress, mount, visibility)` takes the process-visibility analysis beside egress and the mount guard (each None for a guard that did not check the batch) and returns the rerun without every run any of them leaves out when two or more did; a run is kept only when every guard that checked the batch says `clean` or `not_applicable`, so a run the mount guard does not apply to is left out only for another guard. `GuardedRun` gains `visibility`, `left_by` and a `by` that names a guard, says `both` for both guards of a batch of two, or joins the names with `+`; the counts gain `visibility_only` and `several`. The section is named for the guards that checked the batch (`Egress and mount isolation together` for egress and the mount guard, whose text, keys and counts are unchanged; `Egress, mount isolation and process visibility together` for all three) and follows every single guard's section; `analysis.json` `guards` gains the guards that checked the batch and each run's `visibility`; the terminal says `all guards:` for three.
- A preregistration declares the sensitivity reruns it expects (S19). `[sensitivity] reruns = ["egress", "mount_guard", "process_visibility", "any_guard"]` (each at most once, in any order; `reruns = []` declares none) is read by the loader (`expcore.stats.reruns`, `Preregistration.sensitivity_reruns`), and `analyse_study` refuses unless the reruns it would run are exactly those, in either direction, naming each declared rerun no record supports (with why) and each rerun that would run and is not declared; a rerun counts as run when its guard checked the batch, whether or not it left a run out. A preregistration with no table reads as `reruns = []`: a batch no guard checked runs no rerun and passes, its `analysis.json` unchanged (one with the table has `sensitivity_reruns` in it), and a batch a guard checked is refused, naming the reruns it would run and the table that is missing. A study that lists a guard's instrument now declares its reruns; the repository's own guarded studies (the HPC rehearsal and the guard pipeline tests) do.
- The rootless Onedata bundle for HPC batch nodes, `deploy/hpc-federation` (#179, O53): one Onezone or Oneprovider per batch job and node, in a setuid Apptainer writable sandbox, on nodes with no network namespaces and a `/tmp` shared with other users. A default-deny LD_PRELOAD port shim keeps every port but 8443 and 6665 on loopback, with an owner check against other uids. Couchbase's administrator is a per-launch random password of 24 alphanumerics, the most the image's `couchbase-cli` accepts (a longer one made `cluster-init` skip the administrator with exit status 0 in the third live run), and a guard right after onepanel's Couchbase setup stops the service unless this launch's administrator answers `GET /settings/web` and an anonymous request is refused. Site specifics come from a local `site.env` that is never committed. What has run live, and what has not, is in the dated facts files under `deploy/hpc-federation/facts/`; the run driver's steps, sanitised, are under `deploy/hpc-federation/run/`.
- An HPC federation brought up and down from the Compose federation's layout file (#179, O57): `deploy/hpc-federation/hpcfed.py --layout L --root R up | down | describe`, with its process model in `deploy/hpc-federation/DESIGN.md`. One held job of `1 + providers` nodes, read back after submit (cancelled by its own id unless Slurm recorded what was asked), and every service and control action a named `srun --jobid --exact` step launched from the login node behind an `env -i` allowlist, never `--overlap`; the zone and each provider the bundle's batch script as a service step, each with every peer's address, each provider registered by a token piped from the zone; a state file in the root (`hpc-federation.json`, no secret) written before each action it enables, so `down` tears every service down, checks each node and cancels the held job by its id after a whole or a partial `up`. The core's phase is the Compose bootstrap over `https://<node FQDN>:8443` (with the LUMA local feed's mappings to the job's uid), the baseline, `platform.toml` from the layout's template (`[faults]` for the HPC backend with the stop's whole bound, and the least `[tokens] margin_s` the platform layer's own loader accepts for it) and the platform layer's link check, whose record lands in the root. When the core has M4's login-node fault relay, `up` starts it last, in an environment of PATH, HOME and LC_ALL alone, and waits for its heartbeat, and `down` stops it first. The layout's `[hpc]` table sizes every step from the live peaks (Compose ignores it); `layouts/pilot-shape.toml` has the pilot's shape with invented names and ten link pairs. `[hpc] core = "step"` runs the core's phase in a step on the zone's node instead of the login node. A stable hook (`hpcfed_control.run`) carries the service verbs `stop`, `start`, `status` and `exec`. **Not run live.**
- The HPC federation's agent's node held at its own shape (#316 follow-up, O57): `[hpc] sandbox_placement = "het"` (the default) holds it as component 1 of a Slurm heterogeneous job (`sbatch <services> : <agent>`), each component read back on its own and every step on the agent's node run with `--het-group=1`; `"separate"`, the fallback, holds it as a second held job (`<allocation>-sandbox`), read back and cancelled exactly like the first. Its size comes from `[hpc] sandbox` or, when the layout's platform template declares the Apptainer sandbox, from the agent's limits (its cores + 2, its memory, work and tmp bytes + 4 GiB; a smaller `[hpc] sandbox` is refused). The state file's new top-level `sandbox` record names its node, job and het group, which M5's shard step takes; `down` releases both components or both jobs after a whole or a partial `up`. The service nodes keep their 5-core 11 GB shape, so the pilot shape with an 18 GB agent's node bills about 42 units per hour against 63 for one uniform 18 GB shape. **Changed:** `[hpc] sandbox` no longer enlarges every node of the held job. **Not run live** (`sbatch --test-only` accepted such a job on the site).
- Provider outages and the link heal on an HPC federation (O58): `[faults] backend = "hpc"` builds `expcore_onedata.fault_hpc.HpcDriver`, the Compose driver's surface over four verbs of the federation (`status` with a launch identity and readiness, `stop` keeping the work directory and registration, `start`, `exec` of the heal's `ping` and `restart_link` inside the provider's Apptainer service), so the injector, its journal, the restore, the heal and the fault gate run unchanged. A campaign on a node reaches them through the fault relay (`expcore_onedata.fault_relay`), a login-node process `up` starts and `down` stops, with owner-only request and answer files on the federation's root; it runs only its own federation's verbs, never replays an earlier relay's request, and an unanswered request is unknown, never done. A dead relay refuses an outage run at its admission, uncharged. `stop_bound_s` (690 s), `relay_wait_s` (30 s), `settle_timeout_s` 750 s refused at or below the stop's bound, `poll_s` 10 s; the least `[tokens] margin_s` is 5,340 s with the defaults. The stop's duration and end are journalled with the injection. The bundle gains `teardown.sh --keep`, `restart.sh`, `exec.sh`, `status.sh` and a kept work directory the sweep honours, and `deploy/hpc-federation/hpcfed_control.py` the four verbs over the federation's launcher and state. **Wired to the HPC driver (O57):** `hpcfed.py stop|start|status|exec` run the verbs over its own launcher and state file, and `up` starts this core's relay and `down` stops it. A control step whose end the launcher cannot read is never a success; the allocation's end (Slurm's `EndTime`) is read in the login node's zone, as `scontrol` printed it; a stop is confirmed only when no start's pending step can still be made, and its record never undoes a later start; a start follows a stop whose verdict was never recorded once nothing runs, but never launches beside a live process or a launch in flight, and a stop or a start holds the federation's verbs lock, so two never interleave. The relay refuses a request id it has taken before (`replayed`) and a field of the wrong type or a bound that is no finite number (`malformed`) without stopping, and a verb that fails once it may have run is answered unknown, never `not_run`. A restarted owner of shared imported trees is ready only once a status has proven them read-only for its launch, with the shared import's own check (`hpcimport.mount_check_argv` on its node, O59). No verb acts on the agent's node (`services.sandbox`), and the relay lists the providers alone. Every stop's evidence, confirmed or not, gives the verb's `seconds` and the service's own `service_seconds`, in the verb's answer, the state file and the journal (`stop_unknown` too), the figure `[hpc] stop_seconds` is to be set from. `supervise.py` makes the pidfd calls itself (434, 424) on a Python built without them. Nothing of it has run live.
- Egress detection where the agent's network cannot be restricted (#179, O52; `expcore.egress`). On an HPC batch node without network namespaces the agent's cell loads a logging shim (`deploy/hpc-federation/bundle/egresslog.c`) whose records go to a collector outside the cell (`egress-collector.py`); the instrument `EgressLog` (namespace `egress`) records each run's destinations by class (the data gate, loopback or outside, from the run's declaration) and its outside destinations, and a log that is missing, damaged, of another run or without the launcher's canary is unknown, never clean. The agent's code cells are scanned for URLs off the platform, downloads and network calls. Once one analysed record carries the instrument, `analyze` judges every analysed run (a record without the snapshot is unknown), runs the preregistered analysis again without the flagged runs, and the report (`## Egress (detected, not prevented)`), `analysis.json` (`egress`) and the terminal show both, the sensitivity analysis with each named Holm family's and each declared equivalence family's table, steps and place in `analysis.json` (`holm_families`, `equivalence_families`) as the primary analysis shows them, as do the mount guard's and both guards' reruns (S15, S17, S18); a batch without it is unchanged byte for byte. Detection, not prevention: a statically linked program, raw system calls, io_uring and the C library's resolver pass the shim. Not run live yet.
- A tree on the cluster's shared filesystem can be the data of a space in several federations at once, read-only in every one (O59; `docs/storage-import.md`, "A tree on the cluster's shared filesystem"; `deploy/hpc-federation`). `import = { readonly = true, shared = true }` in `platform.toml` declares it (`shared` is refused at load beside `readonly = false`, is in the fingerprint only when true, and `ImportedSpace.check_storage` refuses a storage whose `readonly` is not the boolean `true`, before the scan is read; the record carries `shared`).
