The smallest-effect rule (#81) and several Holm families (#76), which #138 says it must work with, are not in this release; a ratio contrast enters the one Holm family as any contrast does.
- Ended transfers cannot be removed: Oneprovider 25.0's REST API offers no call that deletes one, so a run's agent can list the transfers of earlier runs, the reset's own included. The reset records instead what the listing shows, the count and the newest finish time per provider and together, so an analysis can account for it. Finding QoS requirements reads one summary per leaf of the space, because the API lists them only per entry. The day-0 checks still read the one space, so they refuse a configuration with several.
- The progressive ingest's arrival mode ran on the Compose federation on 12 Nov; its preallocated mode has not run live, so the order it extends a file in (create, empty write at the final size, then the prefix) is measured only the other way round (prefix, then the empty write, on 10 Nov) and traced in the source. Both lack what the issue builds on #27: its sanction, recovery machinery (the preallocated mode has its baseline marks and `recover()`, which a person runs when the start's own recovery could not settle a failure; nothing schedules it) and exclusive admission with a matched control (a study that runs other conditions on the same owner's space must keep them apart itself until then), and optional metadata signals of an ingest in progress. A part is confirmed before the next is written, so a provider slow to show a part makes the next one late, and the reading says by how much; the preallocated start removes, waits out and makes again one file after another, so a plan of many files has a long start, before the agent's first turn. What a live check must show is in [`docs/interfaces.md`](docs/interfaces.md#instrument).
- An upload decides to skip or resume by reading the target back and hashing it, and verifies by reading the whole file back again, so a resumed or new upload moves its bytes about twice over the network; Onedata 25.0 exposes no checksum to compare instead. A file that changes during the upload fails the check rather than being reported. The large-file live test (`tests/live/test_contract.py`, with `EXPCORE_LIVE_MUTATE=1`) has not been run. An upload over a longer target the provisioner made is refused rather than truncated (O44): remove the file first.
- The day-0 outage check has run live: on 12 Nov its first run came out unknown and its rerun passed (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-12-federation-facts-provider-outage-rerun.md`), and what the rerun measured is in its facts file. The tests' fakes' answers while a provider is down (a read that blocks, a transfer that stays active, a tool's wording) are still a stand-in's choices, not measurements, and their stand-in tool server relays the four tools to the fakes, so the real server's arguments and answers are read only from its own input schema and its text. A tool whose required argument the check cannot fill from its schema is not called and leaves the check unknown.
- The package build's `git diff` (O81, `package/tracking.py`) still refreshes the author's `.git/index` when a tracked file's mtime changed, on git 2.43: `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` does not stop that verb. It is a build step on the author's repository, not a launch check, and a check that must not write reads with `git status`.
- Not in this release: streaming transport (B17), snapshot cut and fetch (C21), confirmed model-level faults (C22), blind coding sheets (S08), the package scanner's canaries (K02) and its removal of temporary credential copies (K09), two-host execution, sampling for cause coding and further estimators ([`docs/architecture.md`](docs/architecture.md#release-boundary)).
- The writing tool-server token carries no `api` caveat either. Onedata's `api` caveat could narrow the token to named operations without limiting it to data access, but which operations the tool server's calls are is not in Onedata's caveat source, and one missing entry would refuse a tool, so the token has none. What the live run showed is in O05's entry of [`docs/regressions.md`](docs/regressions.md).
- A writing condition's tool-server token ran live on 12 Nov (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-12-federation-facts-tool-server-write.md`): in its space its replication completed and its QoS requirement was accepted, outside it both were refused, and the three reads below answered 200. The read of its user's Onezone admin privileges, `GET /users/{id}/effective_privileges`, is named by Onezone's REST description alone (`client.UNVERIFIED_ENDPOINTS`), which says its reader needs `oz_view_privileges`: an admin credential without that privilege refuses every writing token. The read of the clusters the token's user is a member of, `GET /user/effective_clusters`, is named by that description and by oz-worker's route alone (`client.UNVERIFIED_ENDPOINTS`); it is the current user's, sent as the token, since the description has no read of another user's. What the live run showed is in O05's entry of [`docs/regressions.md`](docs/regressions.md).
- A read-only condition's tool-server token is refused the transfer and QoS-requirement views by Onedata 25.0 (the live run of 12 Nov of #177, `deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-12-federation-facts-tool-server-write.md`). Every write is refused and the read tools under `/data/{id}` (file distribution, QoS summary, attributes) serve, but `get_transfer`, `list_space_transfers` and `get_qos_requirement` answer 401 `tokenCaveatUnverified`, because 25.0 takes the token's `data.readonly` and `data.path` caveats, which #177 leaves as they were, as a limit to data access. The tool server's own examination of that token is refused too, so the server fails open on its write tools, which Onedata still refuses. A known gap, tracked in #181.
- A cell's deadline reaches the agent's cells only: a harness step (`exec(..., record=False)`, the start's read-only probes among them) starts no timer, and is bounded by the container's `timeout` backstop alone. The sidecar's abort of the FUSE connection (fusectl mounted in its own namespace, its SYS_ADMIN) has not run on a live federation yet; where it fails, the stop does the rest and the attempt is still set aside. A study whose outage condition has its agent read, through the mount, a file only the stopped provider holds will see each such attempt set aside as `cell_unkillable` and run again into the same wait: bounding the read is the study's choice (oneclient's `--provider-timeout`, through `[sandbox.mount] extra_args`, unmeasured and not enabled).
- The HPC mount guard (O60) detects, it does not prevent: inotify names no process, so every event on the token's file or directory while cells can run counts as the agent's, and it never sees a read of memory the file was mapped into; oneclient's tracer is sampled every 50 ms, so a short attach is missed; oneclient's environment and memory are covered only by the proof that no cell can see its process; a cell that starts and ends between two polls is not proved itself (the run needs one proved cell, and every cell has the same command line); and a process of the uid outside the cells (an ssh login into the node, a job step) is outside every check, so no interactive login and no other job step of the account may run on a sandbox node while runs are live (the study guide's rule, for egress too). Whether setuid Apptainer binds a FUSE mount point made without `allow_other`, and a oneclient that runs natively on a node, are untested live.
- The Apptainer backend's check of what `/proc` hides (O56) detects; it is no cgroup per container. Hidden memory under `hidden_memory_margin_bytes` is not told from the node's own hidden share (Apptainer's setuid starter, the cell's init, the kernel's batched counters); a hidden process's shared memory (a memfd, a shared anonymous mapping) is not compared, since the cgroup counts it with the run's tmpfs files; pages a visible process shares with its forked children count once per process in the visible sum, so they can mask as much; and a process forked twice under the cell's hidden init escapes the `hidden_process` signal. The step's memory limit bounds what is missed, and every hidden process ends with its cell's PID namespace. The default margin (128 MiB, about three times the whole overhead the probe's step cgroup showed) and the cgroup's `anon` on the site's nodes are untested live.
